## 142. Closed 2026-11-06, scheduled operator cycle: a 0-byte Etsy listing image, committed since 2026-10-14 and silently re-served as broken on every run since, found and fixed, plus the gate that could not have caught it

The stop hook after entry 141's push reported an uncommitted change to `build/listings/etsy/L4-moving-in/listing-images/6S-Moving-In-Kit-1-first-page.png`. Investigated rather than discarded it: `git show HEAD:<path> | wc -c` read 0, and `git ls-tree -l HEAD` confirmed the committed blob is the literal git empty-blob hash, while its two siblings are committed at their real sizes and already matched the working tree. One of three Etsy listing preview images for a real, soon-to-be-published product (`OWNER-ACTIONS.md` item 4, issue #44) has been a 0-byte blank file since commit `847beec22`, ironically titled "Fix gate_etsy_pdfs_current leaving preview PNGs dirty." Root cause: `check_etsy.py`'s listing-images check only ever counted directory entries, so a 0-byte file counted the same as a real photo every run, and `gate_etsy_pdfs_current`'s own cleanup restores every touched file under the Etsy tree back to its committed (broken) state after each run, hiding the defect behind its own tidiness. Verified the regenerated file (`PIL.Image.verify()`: valid PNG, 4167x3125, matching both siblings' dimensions) and committed its real content. Strengthened `check_etsy.py` to open and verify every listing image, not just count them. Fail-then-pass proved directly against the real file: zeroed it, watched the new check fail by name, restored, confirmed clean. Swept the whole repository for the same defect class (`git ls-tree -r -l HEAD`, zero-byte blobs): this was the only one. `fix_dashes.py --check` 0/0, `py_compile` clean, `test_gate_etsy_listing_valid.py` (4/4), `test_check_etsy_free_duplicate.py` (6/6), `test_check_etsy_stale_economics.py` (5/5) all pass unaffected. No price or product touched; `IndexNow` not applicable (not a site page). Full account in `ops/NIGHTLY-LOG.md`.

## 139. Closed 2026-11-06, PM check-in (06:1x slot): the DST shift three workflows' own headers warned would "bite in November" had already bitten, five days unapplied, fixed in all three

PM triage, not the 30-minute re-verification cadence this slot usually produces. Attached per Step 0: fetch, real unshallow from a shallow detached checkout, `checkout -B main origin/main`, ff only onto `9a08b7db7`, tree clean. Previous work (entry 138's `linkedin-drafts.yml` re-fit, and the 05:4x PM check-in's room-guide handoff) confirmed finished, not cited: `preflight.py --skip-tests` run fresh to a log file, 0 FAIL through every gate reached, stalled genuinely (flat `ps`, no new log line) at the documented network-bound `gate_etsy_pdfs_current`, killed, reported unchecked past that point. `cold_read_ledger.py --stale` 0 stale. `check_urls.py` 235/235. `fix_dashes.py --check` 0/0. Deploy gap re-derived directly with `resolve_verdict_commit`/`deploy_gap_material_commits`: 0 material commits undeployed. GitHub re-pulled live: 29 open issues, unchanged, all `decision`/`blocked-on-art`; issue #42's comment thread re-read directly, nothing from Phil since 2026-11-02. 0 open PRs.

**The real finding, not a re-verification.** Entry 138 (this same morning, ~05:4x) re-fit `linkedin-drafts.yml`'s queueing-lag compensation but left its own header's separate, older "TIMEZONE, and this will bite in November... Add an hour to the cron then" warning untouched. `date -d "2026-11-01" +%A` confirms 2026-11-01 was a Sunday, the first in November, so Denver switched from MDT to MST that day. Today is 2026-11-06, five days later. Grepped the whole repo for "Denver" in `.github/workflows/`: three files, not one, carry the same unresolved warning: `linkedin-drafts.yml`, `owner-questions.yml`, `roadmap-report.yml`. All three had been quietly arriving an hour earlier than their own stated intent since 2026-11-01, and nothing flagged it, because `gate_scheduled_delivery_phase` only checks a workflow's measured landing against its own stated UTC promise, not against what that promise is supposed to mean in Denver local time; the DST compensation was always a human-maintained note, not a gated one.

**Fixed in all three, not just re-noted:** `linkedin-drafts.yml`'s `# lands-at` promise moved from 14:19 UTC to 15:19 UTC and its cron from `09 9 * * *` to `09 10 * * *` (same 310.2-minute compensation entry 138 measured, now one hour later in UTC to land at the same 08:19 Denver). Critically, this workflow also has a push-trigger fallback that hardcodes the deadline as `date -u -d 'today 14:19'` in actual shell logic, not just a comment; left at 14:19 it would have started standing in for the schedule an hour before the schedule's own new 15:19 UTC target, reopening the exact double-send bug (`CHECKIN-LOG.md`/this file's own history of 8-of-28 days with 2-3 sends) the fallback already exists to prevent. Updated that line to `15:19` in the same commit. `owner-questions.yml`'s weekly cron moved from `17 14 * * 1` to `17 15 * * 1` (no fallback logic to touch). `roadmap-report.yml`'s four crons moved from `41 14/18/23/3` to `41 15/19/0/4`; critically, this workflow also matches `github.event.schedule` against the literal cron string in a `case` statement to label which edition is sending (the exact fix for the 2026-09-19 bug its own comments describe, where a wall-clock read mislabelled every edition as "Morning"), so the four cron strings in that `case` block were updated in the same commit, or every edition would have silently fallen through to the `*` default and reported as edition 8 regardless of which one actually ran.

**Verified:** `yaml.safe_load` parses all three edited files. `check_cron_cadence.intended_landing('linkedin-drafts.yml')` reads back `(15, 19)` and `scheduled_times()` reads back `[(10, 9)]`, confirming the promise and the cron moved together. `test_gate_roadmap_edition_from_schedule.py` (5/5), `test_gate_schedule_comment_minute.py` (6/6, including `test_real_committed_files_are_clean` against the files as they now stand), `test_check_cron_cadence.py` (12/12) all pass; these are synthetic-fixture tests that do not read the real cron hours, confirmed by reading their source, so passing proves the gate shape still works, not that today's specific hours are right, which the direct `intended_landing`/`scheduled_times` read above covers instead. `py_compile` on `preflight.py` and `check_cron_cadence.py`. `fix_dashes.py --check` 0/0. `check_urls.py` 235/235. Grepped the whole repo again post-fix for the old cron strings (`41 14`, `41 18`, `41 23`, `41 3 `, `17 14 * * 1`) outside archives and test fixtures: none remain in a real workflow.

**Not verified, honestly:** whether these actually land at 08:19/08:17 Denver going forward still depends on GitHub's queueing staying near the medians measured today, same caveat entry 138 already states, and `gate_scheduled_delivery_phase` will read `linkedin-drafts.yml` as "not yet verified" for a few days against the newly-shifted cron, the same expected transition entry 138 produced. `owner-questions.yml` and `roadmap-report.yml` carry no `# lands-at` marker and no gate checks their local-time accuracy at all; this fix is correct by hand-derivation (UTC offset arithmetic), not by a gate turning green, and a future DST transition will need the same manual catch again in March 2027 unless a gate is built for it, which is future work, not this slot's. Each file's header now names 2027-03-14 (the second Sunday in March, when Denver returns to MDT) as the revert date. No price, product or site page touched; `IndexNow` not applicable. Full account in `ops/NIGHTLY-LOG.md`.

Attached per Step 0 (shallow and detached on arrival, `git fetch --unshallow`, `checkout -B main origin/main`, ff only onto `c7e1af2e8`, tree clean, 1229 commits fast forwarded). The most recent entry (2026-11-06 ~00:1x PM check-in) reread sections 2 through 6 of this file directly and found only C5 and C6 unstruck, both Phil gated, and named entry 135's own `zone_faq()` gap as the one remaining real thread, deferred rather than closed.

**Finished the inventory entry 135 named as "the next cycle's first step."** Entry 135 had grepped five durability keywords across the 20 rooms' own `sustain` pass text and found one genuine hit (Nursery) plus three false positives, but explicitly said a keyword grep is not the right method (CLAUDE.md 5c, a count is not a finding) and that a human read per zone was still owed. Beyond the already known Nursery diaper backstock line, found six more lines that genuinely describe something going bad, wearing out, or needing a date check over time: Pantry Baking Zone (leaveners losing their lift), Primary Bathroom Vanity Drawers (a dried out cosmetic looking identical to a good one), Primary Bathroom Medicine Cabinet (reading expiry dates on every pharmacy trip), Home Office File Storage (a tax retention period), Garage Automotive Care Zone (brake fluid and coolant changing once opened), and Workshop Safety and PPE Station (dated checks on the extinguisher bracket and first aid kit).

None of the six candidates appear anywhere in either file. **Decision: do not build the generic `zone_faq()` sustain question.** A schema change reaching every one of 114 zones' FAQPage structured data, in a sandbox that cannot run `gate_tests` to completion (confirmed again this cycle, same documented headless Chromium and no egress hang, `test_audit_catalog.py`), to recover exactly one already partially covered, low signal autocomplete query, is not a reasonable trade. This is not the same shape as A32's "do not fabricate a heading claim the content does not make," because the content genuinely supports the Nursery answer; it is a proportionality call, the kind CLAUDE.md 0.2 still expects an operator to make rather than default to building anything merely because a gap is real. Closing the thread with that decision, not another deferral, is itself the action this slot took: entries 131, 134 and 135 each handed this cluster forward once more; this one ends it.

## 135. Closed 2026-11-05, scheduled operator cycle: closed entry 134's own last thread ("how long do diapers last in storage"), found it is a mechanism gap rather than a one-row fix, named the mechanism rather than built it blind

**Did:** Attached per Step 0 (arrived shallow and detached, real `git fetch --unshallow`, `checkout main`, ff-only onto `origin/main` at `57babf5c5`, tree clean throughout). Read `BACKLOG-2026-09-07.md`, `ROADMAP-2026-2029.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `GOALS.md`, the last four `ops/NIGHTLY-LOG.md` entries (confirmed newest-first by `git log`, not by `tail`, per entry 132's own note). GitHub's 29 open issues re-pulled fresh via `gh api search/issues` (the GitHub MCP server failed to connect this session, `CLIENT_HTTP_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`; used the documented search-API exception instead): unchanged set, newest activity still issue #42 at 2026-11-02T13:51:51Z, no reply from Phil. `ops/inbox_agent.py --apply`: no mail credential, unchecked, not assumed empty. `ops/cold_read_ledger.py --stale`: 0 of 308. `ops/deploy-verdict.json` already current (`4f1bb0f700085b1e`, checked 23:19:57Z) and confirmed against this same HEAD, not re-derived stale. Tested this sandbox's own egress directly: `6s-success.com` refused at the proxy (same standing no-egress limit every recent cycle reports), `api.github.com` reachable, confirmed by two real `curl` calls rather than assumed either way. `preflight.py --fast` run fresh to a file: 0 FAIL/ERROR through 119 gates reached, genuinely stalled inside `gate_tests` at `test_audit_catalog.py` (9/473), confirmed by sampling CPU time four times over 70s (flat ~8% total, a live child process not pegged, I/O wait not computation); killed by hand rather than waited out, `/tmp/.preflight_py_running.lock` (pid 1805, confirmed dead) cleared. Reported unchecked past that point per `CLAUDE.md` 0.4.

**Verified before concluding, not assumed from the handoff's own wording.** Ran the real scorer directly against the live corpus rather than trust entry 134's description: `content_words("how long do diapers last in storage")` returns exactly `['long', 'diapers', 'last', 'storage']`, and `page_inventory()`'s own `head_words` for every Nursery page shows the zone page (`/zones/nursery-the-diaper-and-care-backstock.html`) already carries `diapers` and `storage` in its heading surface (2 of 4, 0.5) but neither `last` nor `long`, while the current winning page (a Primary Bathroom article, via a generic FAQ heading every room article shares) scores 0.75 (3 of 4) on a coincidence. A heading-word fix needs the zone to reach at least a tie (0.75), which needs one of `last`/`long` to appear in a heading somewhere on that page, and neither is a zone name or a household synonym for the zone itself (unlike "diapers zone"/"nappies zone"), so there is no honest `also-called` entry to add; per the A32 lesson this entry's own predecessor already applied to "closet," naming a heading claim the content does not make is the wrong fix even if it would move the number.

**Found the real shape of the gap, not a quick synonym miss.** The zone's own `sustain` pass text genuinely answers this question ("Backstock here has a shelf life the pack does not print, because your baby grows while the packs sit still"), rendered in full on the page body, but `ops/build_zone_pages.py`'s `zone_faq()` (the function that turns page content into FAQPage headings/structured data) only ever builds questions from five fixed fields: `session`, `done_looks_like`, `the_call`, `leave_behind`, and `shine_detail` (products/first surface) plus `watch_for`. `sustain` text is not one of them, by design (its docstring: "Nothing here is written for this function; every answer is the same sentence a reader sees... A zone with none of these fields returns an empty list"). So no zone's `sustain` text can ever appear in a heading, which means a "how long does X last" or "how do you know when to restock X" class of query can never score above a coincidental tie anywhere on the site, regardless of how directly the real content answers it. **Checked whether this is wider than Nursery, and caught a false positive before writing it down as a finding:** a first keyword grep for durability language ("shelf life," "goes stale," "expires," "use by," "spoil") across all 20 rooms' `sustain` text returned 4 hits; reading each in context (not just counting them, per the A41/count-is-not-a-finding lesson) showed 3 of the 4 are coincidental substring matches whose actual claim is the opposite of durability (Pantry: "cans never force the issue by spoiling"; Mudroom: "wet... never expires on its own"; Home Office: no real "use by" phrase present at all, a cross-word substring accident). Only Nursery's own match ("a shelf life the pack does not print") is genuine. So the mechanism gap in `zone_faq()` is confirmed real, but its actual reach beyond Nursery is not yet known and is not asserted here; a proper inventory needs a human-read pass per zone, not a keyword grep, and is the next cycle's first step if this is picked up.

**Deliberately not built this cycle.** A new `zone_faq()` question sourced from `sustain` text would touch all 114 zones' structured data (FAQPage JSON-LD) at once, not one row, and this sandbox's `gate_tests` cannot run to completion (the documented headless-Chromium/no-egress hang), so there is no way to prove a schema-surface change like this fail-then-pass against the full suite before shipping, the same bar every other structured-data fix logged this week was held to. Building it anyway, unverified, on a surface search engines parse directly, is a worse outcome than leaving one query unrouted. Not attempted blind.

**Went well:** reading `zone_faq()`'s own source and docstring before writing a single line caught that this is a five-zone-plus mechanism gap, not a one-row heading miss, before any code was touched.

**Did not go well:** none; the right call here was recognizing the ceiling on what this slot could safely verify, not a mistake made and caught.

**Changing next cycle:** none yet; the next cycle that picks this up should first finish the `sustain`-language inventory across all 20 rooms (started above, not completed), then design the new `zone_faq()` question wording against 2-3 real examples, prove the digital-collision check and the full existing FAQ test suite still pass before touching a single zone, and only then roll it out, the same sequence every multi-zone fix this week has used.

**Next:** this closes the standing "diapers" cluster handoff from entries 131/134; nothing further is actionable in it without the `zone_faq()` work above. Otherwise unchanged: 29 open `decision`-labelled GitHub issues, no reply from Phil since 2026-11-02; issue #53's G2 gate date has still arrived with no budget/stop-date decision; not re-notifying this cycle, since nothing material has changed since the 2026-11-02T04:17Z push (same issue count, no Phil reply, revenue still $0 of $20,000). No price, product, or page touched; no generator run; `BACKLOG-2026-09-07.md` (this entry), `ops/NIGHTLY-LOG.md`, dashboard trio. `IndexNow` not applicable, no new page.

**"plan" (21 rows, both tiers): checked for self-flattery, found a different intent instead.** 0 of 212 pages carry the bare word "plan" in a title or h1-h3 heading (grepped directly), so there is no text-overlap self-flattery risk the way the file's own rule for `FORMAT_WORDS` describes. **Two false starts caught before shipping, not after.** First tried "understairs cupboard": rebuilt clean, but rescoring both tiers moved zero rows, because `keyword_demand.py`'s own `words()` tokenizer treats "understairs" as one token, never equal to the query's own "stairs" token, the literal-word-not-stem bar A34/A36 already learned for nappy/nappies. Added to Hall Closet's `names` in `ops/room-also-called.json` with both false starts recorded in the entry's own `source` field so a future cycle does not repeat either. Ran `ops/build_zone_pages.py` (the generator that owns this heading), not a hand edit.

Found and removed one stray artifact before it could skew a check: a `_contact_form_interactive_probe.html` file left in `site/` by the killed `gate_tests` run was briefly making `audit_pages.py` report 5 findings on an untracked "hand written page"; removed, reran, 240/0 clean. `check_urls.py` 235/235. `link_graph_report.py` 0 orphans. `check_sellable.py` 124/126 unchanged (2 known service SKUs). `audit_visual.py` on `site/rooms/hall-closet.html`, desktop and mobile, 0 findings each. `preflight.py --skip-tests` run fresh to a file after all of the above: 0 FAIL through every gate reached (all 29 of the Etsy-listing gates included), genuinely stalled at `gate_etsy_pdfs_current` (a live Etsy fetch; its own sibling gate in the same run logged the fee-schedule 403 this sandbox always gets), reported unchecked past that point, not assumed passing. ## 124. Closed 2026-11-05, scheduled operator cycle: a second real cross-room measurement-attribution bug fixed (guide articles winning on a bag-of-words combination from two unrelated headings), not another vocabulary-gap mine

Attached per Step 0 (fetch, real unshallow from a shallow detached checkout, `checkout -B main origin/main`, ff-only onto `0e6ac08e6`, tree clean, 1120 commits fast-forwarded). Read `GOALS.md`, this file sections 0-7, `ROADMAP-2026-2029.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, the nightly-log's last four entries (121-123, covering Primary Bedroom, Home Office, Stair Landing). `preflight.py --skip-tests` run fresh before touching anything: 0 FAIL through every gate reached, stalled at the documented network-bound `gate_etsy_pdfs_current`. GitHub re-pulled directly: 29 open issues, all `decision`, unchanged since 2026-11-02T13:51:51Z, none from Phil.

Picked up entry 123's own handoff to check why Workshop's uncovered share had risen from the 24% entry (`A59` in section 4 above) left it at back up to 38% since. Confirmed first, not assumed: none of A59's four fixed queries had regressed (all still `covered`); the rise was new keyword-harvest rows landing, not a regression, as entry 123 itself suspected. While reading Workshop's current 23 partial rows against real content to confirm that (CLAUDE.md 5c/5d), found `garage organization ideas for small garages` labeled `room: "Garage"` in the harvest's own data but routed to `/articles/how-to-organize-a-workshop.html`. Opened the real page headings (not the row's printed summary) for both guide articles: Workshop's own guide combines "Step 4: The fastener and hardware zone" (contributes "hardware") with a separate heading "A workshop that lives in the garage" (contributes "garage"), two genuinely different, real, non-boilerplate headings on one page. `keyword_demand.py`'s heading scorer pools every heading's words into one bag with no requirement that a match come from a single coherent heading, so the two together scored this one page 1.0 on `['garage','hardware']`-type queries, beating Garage's own on-topic zone pages outright, not on a tie.

Scanned the full corpus for the same shape before touching anything (not reporting a count as a finding, CLAUDE.md 5c): any row whose own room signal (`household_room_signal`) disagreed with the room that actually owns its winning guide article, where that room's own zone page already independently cleared the 0.5 partial threshold. Found 6 distinct queries this way (9 rows across both harvest files), including two already-`covered` false claims read in full before fixing anything: `how to organize hardware in garage` and `how to organize power tools in garage`, both `room: "Garage"`, both scored a false 1.0 "covered" on Workshop's guide article while Garage's own Power Tool and Battery Zone and Lawn and Garden Tool Zone genuinely cover the same ground at 0.5-0.667. A third pair, `small guest bathroom decorating/design/ideas`, carried the identical shape against Hall Closet's own guide article (which separately combines "small" and "guest bathroom"-adjacent wording across two of its own headings) while Guest Bathroom's own Guest Linen Zone already answers directly.

**Root cause, not symptom:** `room_of()` in `ops/keyword_demand.py` deliberately gave every article no room at all ("the common case," since most articles answer no single room), which also meant the 20 curated per-room guide articles (`build_zone_pages.ROOM_GUIDES`) never got the room-signal protection zone and deck pages already have, so nothing stopped one room's guide from out-scoring another room's own, more specific zone page on a cross-heading word coincidence.

**Fixed at the root**, not by hand-patching the six queries: added `guide_article_rooms()` to `ops/keyword_demand.py`, a reverse map of `build_zone_pages.ROOM_GUIDES` (20 curated entries only, every other article still correctly carries no room), wired into `room_of()` and `page_inventory()`. Added `_prefer_in_room_zone()`, called from both `best_page()` and `best_by()`: when the winning page is a GUIDE ARTICLE (not a zone, not a deck) belonging to a different room than the query's own room signal, and that signal room has its own ZONE page clearing 0.5 on the same surface being scored, the zone page replaces the article. Scoped narrowly on purpose, matching the precedent this file's own entry 115 and the A55/A56 band-heading revert already set (a small reversible change over a speculative broad one): a weak in-room page never displaces a stronger cross-room one, proved directly by a second test case built the same way.

**Verified the blast radius before trusting it, not after:** a full before/after diff of both harvest files post-rescore showed exactly 4 status changes corpus-wide (all a false `covered` correctly demoted to an honest `partial`: the two Garage/Workshop rows above, one Guest Bathroom/Hall Closet row, and one weak `best way to garden` collision that was noise either way but is now at least in the right room), gap count unchanged (111/111 US, 91/91 global, so no query became newly unanswered), and 339 US + 334 global page-only changes where `best_page` moved but status did not, checked programmatically against every query's own room signal: 12 moved from wrong-room to correct-room, 0 moved from correct-room to wrong-room, and the remaining ~660 were ties between two already-correct same-room pages (a room's own deck page vs its own guide article) now resolved the same alphabetical way every other same-room tie already was, once the article's room stopped being invisible to that check. Fifteen of these were hand-sampled and read individually, not just counted.

New regression test `ops/tests/test_keyword_demand.py` case 22 (27/27 total), fail-then-pass proved directly against the real shape (reverted the fix, watched both new assertions fail with the exact real query and real headings, restored, reconfirmed green), plus the weak-in-room-page counter-case. `room_guide_underperformance.py`: Garage 51% to 52% uncovered (169 on-topic now, +2, honestly worse because two previously-hidden-as-"covered" queries are now correctly counted here as `partial`), Workshop 38% to 38% (60 on-topic now, -1, both rounding to the same percentage). `check_urls.py` (235/235), `fix_dashes.py --check` (0/0), `check_also_called_digital_collision_fast.py` clean, `test_gate_also_called_no_digital_collision.py` (5/5), `test_gate_zone_also_called_is_heading.py` (6/6), `test_room_also_called.py`, `test_room_guide_underperformance.py` (18/18), `test_room_guide_candidates.py` (20/20), `check_sellable.py` (124/126), `affiliate.py --check` (165 documents), `link_graph_report.py` (0 orphans) all clean. `preflight.py --skip-tests` run fresh after: 0 FAIL through every gate reached, stalled only at the same documented network-bound gate as before the change.

**Went well:** scanning the whole corpus programmatically for the exact shape before shipping, instead of hand-fixing the one query that was found first; it turned up five more real instances and one genuine non-finding (`best way to garden`) in the same pass, and the programmatic room-correctness check caught that the fix had zero regressions across 673 changed rows, which eyeballing alone could not have covered.

**Did not go well:** this took most of the slot on one defect; no vocabulary-gap mining happened this cycle. That is the right trade given CLAUDE.md's own priority order (measurement outranks traffic/SEO content work), not a shortfall.

**Changing next cycle:** none; the fix is narrow and tested, no further gate needed beyond the new regression case.

**Next:** re-run the unscoped room ranking before picking the next room to mine; Living Room (37%), Entryway (35%) and Family Room (34%) remain the next un-mined candidates, now against corrected Garage/Workshop/Guest-Bathroom/Hall-Closet numbers. Nothing from the 29 open GitHub issues is unblocked; no reply from Phil since 2026-11-02.

## 122. Closed 2026-11-05, scheduled operator cycle: Home Office's "office supplies" gap closed, and a real scorer defect found and fixed alongside it that had been handing out false "covered" claims across the whole corpus

GitHub re-pulled directly: 29 open issues, all decision or blocked-on-art, unchanged since 2026-11-02, no reply from Phil. Every backlog row Done or owner-gated, matching every recent cycle.

Picked up the 11:2x PM handoff: Home Office (38% uncovered, 0 gap + 30 partial of 79 on-topic) was the next room not yet individually mined. Pulled every partial row straight from `ops/keyword-demand.json` and `ops/keyword-demand-global.json`, not the console's truncated preview, and read each against real content. Most are the established noise classes already named in prior entries (platform/audience names: ikea, pinterest, reddit, teachers, creatives; generic modifiers: ways, tips, productivity, deep, large, diy, interior, minimal, color, genre, aesthetically; two digital-context "files" queries, cricut/hard-drive, about computer files rather than physical office organizing). Two were real and shared one cause: "how to organize office supplies" and "how to organize home office supplies", both missing only the plural word "supplies" against this zone's own title ("How to organize the home office supply cabinet", singular "supply"). The Supply Cabinet zone's own `content.json` purpose ("Hold the consumables this office genuinely burns through in a year") is exactly office supplies, not a coincidental match.

**Checked for collision before shipping, and found a real one** (CLAUDE.md 5c/5d): the bare word "supplies" is heavily overloaded site-wide (cleaning, baking, craft, car, gardening, cricut, prepping supplies, each already correctly routed to its own zone by a qualifying word), so named the phrase "office supplies" rather than the bare noun, matching the Kitchen Sink and Dishwashing Zone's own precedent and reasoning ("nobody calls this the supplies zone"). Rebuilt and re-scored the full corpus before trusting it clean, the same full before/after diff this file's own precedent entries use, and it was not clean: "cabinet to organize cleaning supplies" (already diagnosed by an earlier cycle as unfixable, since "cabinet" alone names seven zones, and deliberately left as an honest `partial`) silently flipped to a false 1.0 "covered" claim on the Supply Cabinet zone. Traced the actual cause rather than just reverting: `site/zones/home-office-the-supply-cabinet.html`'s own "Cleaning it properly, surface by surface" heading, under its Shine pass, is byte-identical across all 114 zone pages (`grep -c` confirmed), so it was silently contributing the word "cleaning" to every single zone's heading-match signal with zero regard for whether that zone has anything to do with cleaning. Adding "supplies" was the word that tipped this one query over the edge from a 2-of-3 tie into a false 3-of-3, but the underlying defect existed before this cycle touched anything and was already inflating other matches corpus-wide.

Fixed at the root in `ops/keyword_demand.py`: added a `BOILERPLATE_HEADINGS` set, currently holding only this one proven phrase, excluded from `page_inventory()`'s heading extraction before the word sets are built. Deliberately narrow, not a blanket heading strip: an earlier attempt this same cycle to drop every page's promotional cross-sell "band" section wholesale was tried and reverted, because those headings vary per room and often carry real zone-naming signal (a room article's own zone list); this boilerplate heading reads identically everywhere and therefore never carries real signal to lose. Added a regression test (`ops/tests/test_keyword_demand.py` case 21) against a throwaway site tree, proving `page_inventory()` keeps a page's real title words while dropping the boilerplate heading's; fail-then-pass proved directly (reverted the filter, watched the new case fail with the leaked word, restored, reconfirmed 26/26).

**Verified:** `ops/zone-also-called.json` entry added, `ops/build_zone_pages.py` rerun (self-chained: canonical links, landmarks, PWA, AVIF, fingerprint, sitemap), heading grep-confirmed on the real committed page. `ops/keyword_demand.py --rescore`: both target queries moved partial to covered; corpus-wide the scorer fix also corrected several other previously-undetected false `covered` claims that had been riding the same free "cleaning" word (`how to organize a kitchen properly`, `how to organize car cleaning supplies` and four close variants, `cleaning supplies storage ideas`, `help with cleaning and organizing`, `how to store books properly`, `how to properly store paint`), each now reading an honest `partial` or redirected to its genuinely correct zone (several "cleaning supplies" queries now correctly land on Hall Closet's Cleaning Supply Zone instead of Garage); two queries about diaper disposal dropped from an undeserved `partial` to an honest `gap`, confirmed by reading the Nursery Changing Station's real headings, which never use the word "dispose". `ops/keyword_demand_global.py --rescore` same shape. `check_also_called_digital_collision_fast.py`, `test_gate_also_called_no_digital_collision.py` (5/5), `test_gate_zone_also_called_is_heading.py` (6/6), `test_room_also_called.py` all clean. `room_guide_underperformance.py --room "Home Office"`: 38% to 35% uncovered, 49 to 51 covered. `check_urls.py` (235/235), `audit_pages.py` (240/0 findings), `fix_dashes.py`/`fix_dialect.py --check` (0/0), `affiliate.py --check` (165 documents), `link_graph_report.py` (0 orphans, min 2 inbound), `check_sellable.py` (124/126 Stripe-matched, 2 known service SKUs) all clean. `preflight.py --skip-tests` run fresh, unbuffered to a file, both before and after: 0 FAIL through every gate reached, genuinely stalled at the documented network-bound Stripe/GitHub-Actions gates near the end (no `.env.secrets`, same standing sandbox limitation as every recent cycle), reported unchecked past that point, not assumed passing.

**Went well:** running the full-corpus before/after diff instead of trusting a clean collision-checker run caught a real false "covered" claim before it shipped, and following the cause instead of just reverting turned one near-miss into a corpus-wide correction.

**Did not go well:** the band-heading experiment tried and reverted this same cycle cost time for a dead end; worth remembering that a heading's wording VARYING per page is what makes it risky to touch, not whether it is inside a promotional section.

**Changing next cycle:** added `BOILERPLATE_HEADINGS` and its regression test as the gate this lesson earns; several other zone-template headings are also verified byte-identical corpus-wide (`Related reading`, `Common items here`, and others) but are left alone until a second real collision is found, per CLAUDE.md's preference for a small reversible change over a speculative broad one.

**Next:** Home Office's remaining 28 partial rows are now individually diagnosed as the established noise classes. Re-run the unscoped room ranking before picking the next room. Nothing from the 29 open issues is unblocked; no reply from Phil.

No price or product page touched; one room page changed (Primary Bedroom), no new page; `IndexNow` attempted on push, refused by the tool itself because this sandbox could not reach the site to confirm the key file is served first.

Picked up entry 120's own handoff: Primary Bedroom ranked next at 40% uncovered (2 gap, 41 partial of 108 on-topic, 65 covered). Pulled the real rows straight from `ops/keyword-demand.json` and `ops/keyword-demand-global.json` via `room_guide_underperformance.py`'s own `score_room()` (the console tool truncates partial rows to 8), and read every one against the room's real content before concluding anything.

Four rows were real and shared one root cause: "master bedroom storage", "master bedroom storage ideas", "master bedroom organization furniture" and "how to organize bedroom furniture" all scored short by exactly one word (storage or furniture), because neither word appeared in any h1-h3 on `/rooms/primary-bedroom.html`, even though the page's own "Room layout" tip already describes the closet's hanging space (the storage) and the dresser and nightstands (the furniture). Checked before landing: no other room's heading carries "storage" except Hall Closet's own H1, and none carries "furniture" at all, so the risk the "plan"/Nursery precedent in this same function warns about (a new word winning outright on the wrong room rather than merely tying) did not apply here; spot-checked every "furniture" query in both keyword files (guest bedroom, kids bedroom, nursery, dining room, family room, living room) and each still ties or loses to its own room's page, resolved correctly by the existing room-signal tie-break. Retitled the heading, scoped to this one room only, from "Primary bedroom layout" to "Primary bedroom layout, furniture and storage" in `ops/build_zone_pages.py`.

The rest of the partial list is the same established noise classes seen in every prior room, checked rather than assumed: "how to organize bags of spices" and "how to organize disposable bags" (0.0 each) are a generic "bags" word collision with the article's own decluttering-checklist language (grab three bags: keep, donate, trash), nothing to do with spices or disposable bags; "organizing your bedroom tips for style"/"...pinterest" (the two gap rows) are the established style/platform-modifier class; "boys bedroom organization ideas" and "childrens bedroom organization ideas" genuinely belong to Kids Bedroom rather than this room, but the literal typed form ("childrens", no apostrophe) is not a word this site can honestly print in a heading, so this stays an open, low-value misattribution rather than a forced fix; the remaining rows are generic declutter-checklist filler ("tips", "guide", "checklist pdf", "near me", "mumsnet", "study", "hallway") that this whole-house-level article was never going to carry and should not be stuffed with.

**Verified:** `ops/build_zone_pages.py` rerun; grep-confirmed the new heading on the real committed `site/rooms/primary-bedroom.html`, and confirmed no other room's heading changed. `keyword_demand.py --rescore`: 4 queries moved partial to covered across two passes (partial 1248 to 1245, covered 1466 to 1469, gap unchanged at 109). `keyword_demand_global.py --rescore`: same shape (partial 919 to 914, covered 1160 to 1165, gap unchanged at 91). All four target queries read back directly from the JSON: `status: covered`, `best_page: /rooms/primary-bedroom.html`, `matched_on: heading`, `unmatched_words: []`. Spot-checked every "furniture" query in both files by hand: `guest-bedroom-deck.html`, `kids-bedroom-deck.html`, `nursery-deck.html`, `dining-room-deck.html`, `family-room-deck.html` and `living-room-deck.html` all kept their own correct page, none shifted to Primary Bedroom. `room_guide_underperformance.py --room Primary Bedroom`: 40% to 36% uncovered, 65 to 69 covered, 41 to 37 partial. GOALS.md's own "small space" (25/12/0/37) and "cheap/budget/DIY" (32/64/4/100) cluster citations re-derived directly from the rescored JSON and found unchanged, so no correction needed there. `fix_dashes.py --check` (0/0). `check_urls.py` (235/235). `python ops/preflight.py --skip-tests` run fresh, unbuffered to a file rather than piped (the piped-timeout trap this file has named before): every gate passed, 34 warnings, all standing documented sandbox gaps (no Stripe/SSH/mail credential, site unreachable, Etsy fee page 403). `inbox_agent.py --apply`: no mail credential, unchecked, not assumed empty.

**Went well:** the "plan"/Nursery precedent already written into this exact function told me what to check before adding a word, not after; spot-checking every furniture query across six other rooms caught the risk directly rather than trusting that a single-word addition is automatically safe.

**Did not go well:** nothing new; the same 29-issue decision backlog and $0 this month are unchanged from every recent cycle.

**Next:** Primary Bedroom's remaining 37 partial rows are individually diagnosed (bags/pinterest/style noise, generic whole-house declutter filler, one genuine but unfixable-without-stuffing Kids Bedroom misattribution). ## 120. Closed 2026-11-05, scheduled operator cycle: a genuine Mudroom vocabulary gap found and fixed at the room level, not a zone; a real diagnostic-tool defect found alongside it and fixed in both ranking scripts

GitHub still carries all 29 open issues as decision or blocked on art, unchanged since 2026-11-02, no reply from Phil. Checked directly rather than cited.

Picked up where entry 119 left off: Mudroom ranked next at 42% uncovered (1 gap, 13 partial of 33 on-topic, 19 covered). Pulled the real rows straight from `ops/keyword-demand.json` and `ops/keyword-demand-global.json` via `room_guide_underperformance.py`'s own `score_room()` (the console tool truncates partial rows to 8; read the function's return value directly instead), and read every one against Mudroom's real content.json zones before concluding anything. The one gap row, "organize outdoor diy lab", is noise: a garbled seed, not a real household phrase, and nothing in this room's content is a lab. Three partial rows ("how to organize outdoor shed", "how to organize your outdoor shed", "outdoor storage buildings for seasonal items") are a real demand cluster but not a Mudroom gap: they were scoring against the Seasonal Outdoor Gear zone purely on the words "outdoor"/"seasonal", but that zone's actual content is gloves, hats and umbrellas, never a garden shed or lawn equipment. Adding "shed" there would be the same false-claim shape entry 119 ruled out for Hall Closet's "paper" collision; the honest answer is that a literal outdoor shed is closer to Garage's Lawn and Garden Tool Zone or Bulk and Overhead Storage, a different room, out of scope for this pass and left unfixed. The rest of the partial rows (pinterest, "shop deals", generic "tips"/"ideas" wording) are the same established brand/platform/listicle noise seen in every prior room.

Two rows were real: "how to organize mudroom lockers" and "mudroom locker organization ideas", both partial at 0.5, already routed correctly to this room (via the word "mudroom" itself) but missing "lockers"/"locker", which appeared in no title or h1-h3 heading anywhere on the site (grep-confirmed site-wide, including the one incidental use of "locker" in the sample-chapters download, which is body prose about a rented self-storage unit, not a heading, and not about this room). A built-in mudroom locker system, an assigned hook plus a cubby or bench per person, is exactly what the Family Hook Zone and Bench and Transition Surface already describe together; it names the whole room's built-in system rather than one specific zone, the same shape as the porch/terrace/backyard fix for Patio or Deck, so it was added at room level in `ops/room-also-called.json` rather than to one zone's entry in `ops/zone-also-called.json`. Both the plural and singular forms were added as separate names because the scorer does no stemming for its primary score (`ops/keyword_demand.py`'s own `_morphology_variants()` docstring: used only for tie-break near-miss credit, never the primary score) and each form matches a different one of the two real queries. `ops/build_zone_pages.py` rerun to render the heading on the real room page.

**A second, independent defect surfaced while checking the result, not invented afterward to pad this entry.** `room_guide_underperformance.py --room Mudroom` after the fix still reported only 19 covered (should have been 21), because its `room_rows()` helper, which decides which rows "belong" to a room, matched a room's deck, zone pages and guide article but never the bare room page itself, `/rooms/<slug>.html`, the exact page `also_called_html()` renders a room-level synonym onto. The two newly-fixed queries, now correctly routed by the scorer to `/rooms/mudroom.html`, were silently dropped from the room's row set entirely rather than credited as covered, understating the fix. The same gap sits in `room_guide_candidates.py`'s sibling `room_rows()` function, and would have equally undercounted every earlier room-level fix that won on the room page (porch/terrace/backyard for Patio or Deck, foyer/entrance hall for Entryway, larder for Pantry, and the rest), not just this one. Fixed both functions to also match `/rooms/<slug>.html`; fail-then-pass proved directly (reverting either fix reproduces the wrong 19-covered reading), and a new permanent check added to each of `ops/tests/test_room_guide_underperformance.py` and `ops/tests/test_room_guide_candidates.py` pinning that a room-page-routed row is now counted. While in both files, also fixed a smaller found-alongside defect: both test files' own final summary line was a hardcoded `"%d/%d passed" % (17, 17)` / `"%d/%d passed" % (15, 15)`, already one check short of the real total before this entry's additions and now two short; replaced both with a live counter in `check()` so the printed total can never again silently drift from what actually ran.

**Verified:** grep-confirmed the new heading on the real committed `site/rooms/mudroom.html`. `keyword_demand.py --rescore`: 2 queries moved partial to covered (partial 1250 to 1248, covered 1464 to 1466, gap unchanged at 109). `keyword_demand_global.py --rescore`: same shape (partial 921 to 919, covered 1158 to 1160). Both target queries read back directly from the JSON: `status: covered`, `best_page: /rooms/mudroom.html`, `matched_on: heading`. `room_guide_underperformance.py --room Mudroom`: 42% to 38% uncovered, 19 to 21 covered, 13 to 12 partial, confirming the room_rows() fix actually credits the gain (before that fix landed, the same rescore showed only 19 covered, 41%, the wrong reading). `ops/tests/test_room_guide_underperformance.py`: 18/18 passed (was 17 checks, hardcoded to print 17, now dynamic). `ops/tests/test_room_guide_candidates.py`: 20/20 passed (was 19, hardcoded to print 15, now dynamic; both pre-existing hardcoded totals were already stale before this entry touched either file). `check_also_called_digital_collision_fast.py` clean against the real site. `test_room_also_called.py`: 11 rooms now carry an alternative name (was 10). `test_gate_also_called_is_heading.py` (6/6), `test_gate_zone_also_called_is_heading.py` (6/6), `test_gate_also_called_no_digital_collision.py` (5/5), `test_check_also_called_digital_collision_fast.py` (its own fail-then-pass self-test, 2/2). `fix_dashes.py --check` (0 em dashes, 0 en dashes). `check_urls.py` (235/235). `check_sellable.py` (124 of 126 buyable products matched, 2 known service SKUs, unchanged). `preflight.py --skip-tests`: 0 FAIL across every gate reached; 24 WARN, all the standing documented sandbox gaps (no Stripe/mail credential, no live egress, deploy-freshness unreachable, the decided deck-print-tier and srt-vs-film gaps); run was cut off by its own time budget partway through the Etsy listing gates (external fetch, HTTP 403 through the proxy), the same known limit named in every recent cycle, reported unchecked past that point rather than assumed clean.

**Went well:** checking the result of a fix against the diagnostic tool itself, rather than trusting its first "19 covered" reading, which is what surfaced the room_rows() gap; a shallower verification would have shipped the content fix and left the ranking tool quietly wrong for every future room-level fix.

**Did not go well:** nothing new; the same 29-issue decision backlog and $0 this month are unchanged from every recent cycle.

**Next:** Mudroom's remaining 12 partial rows are individually diagnosed (outdoor-shed cluster correctly belongs to Garage, not here; the rest is brand/platform/listicle noise). The next room on the ranking not yet mined this cycle should be re-read with `room_guide_underperformance.py` fresh, since the room_rows() fix in this entry changes every room's true count, not just Mudroom's. Nothing from the 29 open issues is unblocked; no reply from Phil.

No price or product page touched; one room page changed (Mudroom), no new page; `IndexNow` to submit on push.

## 119. Closed 2026-11-05, scheduled operator cycle: Hall Closet's remaining partial rows confirmed genuinely exhausted after a full word by word check; the next real candidate was Kitchen, where a genuine vocabulary gap under its own sink zone was found and fixed, including one misrouted query

GitHub still carries all 29 open issues as decision or blocked on art, unchanged since 2026-11-02, no reply from Phil. Checked directly rather than cited. `preflight.py --skip-tests` ran clean before and after, 0 FAIL both times.

Picked up where entry 118 left off: Hall Closet ranked second highest at 44% uncovered. Pulled the real 2 gap and 64 partial rows straight from `ops/keyword-demand.json` and `ops/keyword-demand-global.json`, not a console preview, and read every one against the room's real content before concluding anything. Both gap rows are noise, not content gaps: "how to organize closet tutorial guide" fails only on the format words "tutorial" and "guide", and "organize packing kit guide" carries a seed from an unrelated room ("how to organize backpack station") and fails on "packing" and "guide", neither word this room's content has any reason to carry. Of the 64 partial rows, the overwhelming majority are the already-established unfixable class: brand and platform names (amazon, ikea, pinterest, reddit, dollar tree, marie kondo), geography (canada, near me), year and review modifiers (2024, 2025, review, tips, hacks, quickly), or task-verb noise (diy, way, without). Two rows that looked promising on sight turned out to be false matches, not real gaps: "wrapping paper organizer storage" and "how to organize paper recipes" both route to the Paper and Household Backstock zone on the single word "paper", but that zone's real content is toilet roll, light bulbs and batteries, never gift wrap or recipe cards, so adding either word there would be a false claim, not a fix. "cabinet to organize cleaning supplies" was checked against a real collision risk before being ruled out: "cabinet" already names seven different zones across this corpus (Kitchen's two cabinet zones, the China or Display Cabinet, two Under-Sink Cabinets, the Medicine Cabinet, and Home Office's Supply Cabinet), so naming an eighth would make the word meaningless rather than disambiguating anything. Confirmed genuinely exhausted; no fix shipped for this room this cycle, correctly, rather than forced.

Moved to Kitchen, the next highest at 43% (1 gap, 28 partial of 70 on-topic). Same method: the one gap row is "kitchen organization ideas dollar tree", a brand-name row, unfixable. Of the 28 partial rows, most are the same brand/platform/geography/language class (amazon, ikea, pinterest, youtube, tamil, indian, canada) or generic adjectives no content backs (compact, durable, modern, rustic, efficiency, limited). Two rows stood out as real: "how to organize cleaning products under kitchen sink" and "how to organize cleaning supplies under kitchen sink", both partial at 0.4, already correctly routed to the Sink and Dishwashing Zone via "kitchen" and "sink" in its title, missing only the word "products" or "supplies". A third, "how to organize kitchen cleaning supplies", scored partial at 0.667 but was actually misrouted to the Cooking Zone's stove-area page, which has no cleaning content at all (its real content is oil, salt, pepper and a utensil crock); the real match is the same Sink zone. Checked the zone's own `content.json` before touching anything: its `common_items` already names the exact products kept there, "Dish soap, dishwasher tablets, and one all-purpose cleaner, on a single tray under the sink", so "cleaning products" and "cleaning supplies" are what a household actually calls that tray, not an invented label.

Checked for collision before shipping, the same discipline that ruled out Hall Closet's "cabinet": Hall Closet's own zone is literally named "Cleaning Supply Zone" (singular) and Home Office has a "Supply Cabinet" zone, but this scorer compares exact word forms with no stemming, so the singular "Supply" in either zone's title never ties against the plural "supplies" or "products" a query actually uses, and no query here carries an office signal. Added both phrases as plain names, not the "<word> zone" form, matching the "sink cupboard" precedent already on this same zone, since nobody calls this tray the "supplies zone". `ops/zone-also-called.json` updated (`Kitchen|Sink and Dishwashing Zone`), `ops/build_zone_pages.py` rerun to render the heading on the real page.

**Verified:** grep-confirmed the new heading on the real committed `site/zones/kitchen-the-sink-and-dishwashing-zone.html`. `keyword_demand.py --rescore`: 3 queries moved partial to covered, gap unchanged (109), partial 1253 to 1250, covered 1461 to 1464. `keyword_demand_global.py --rescore`: same shape, partial 924 to 921, covered 1155 to 1158. All three target queries read back directly from the JSON: the two "under kitchen sink" queries now `covered` at the same 0.4 title score (matched on heading), and the misrouted one now points at the Sink and Dishwashing Zone instead of the Cooking Zone. `room_guide_underperformance.py --room Kitchen`: 43% to 39% uncovered, 28 to 26 partial. `check_also_called_digital_collision_fast.py` clean. `test_gate_zone_also_called_is_heading.py` (6/6), `test_gate_also_called_no_digital_collision.py` (5/5), `test_room_also_called.py` all clean. `fix_dashes.py --check` (0/0), `check_urls.py` (235/235), `check_sellable.py` (124/126, unchanged exclusions). `preflight.py --skip-tests` run fresh after the change: 0 FAIL.

**Went well:** checking Hall Closet's near-miss "cabinet" candidate against the full corpus before adding it, which caught a real collision risk (seven existing zones already use that word) that a single-room check would have missed.

**Did not go well:** nothing new; the same 29-issue decision backlog and $0 this month are unchanged from every recent cycle.

**Next:** Kitchen's remaining 26 partial rows are now individually diagnosed, same brand/platform/modifier class as everywhere else. Mudroom (42%) is the next room on this ranking not yet mined this cycle. Nothing from the 29 open issues is unblocked; no reply from Phil.

No price or product page touched; one zone page changed (Kitchen Sink and Dishwashing), no new page; `IndexNow` to submit on push.

## 118. Closed 2026-11-05, scheduled operator cycle: a structural gap found, not a synonym gap, deck pages carry no household-word heading at all; fixed for Patio or Deck (3 queries, "porch"), the rest of its remaining partial rows read and confirmed unfixable

A fork re-checked the standing "room-guide gap mining is exhausted" claim before trusting it: Patio or Deck's own number had been read before entry 115's attribution fix landed, so its real post-fix count was unverified. Live re-run: 52% uncovered, 0 gap + 16 partial of 31 on-topic, the single highest shortfall of all 20 rooms, not exhausted.

Pulled the real 16-row list straight from `ops/keyword-demand.json` plus `ops/keyword-demand-global.json` (CLAUDE.md 5c/5d), not the console's own preview. Three queries ("back porch organization ideas", "back porch organizer", "back porch organization") sat at 0.5 coverage with `porch` as the only unmatched word, routed to `/patio-or-deck-deck.html`. The room page already carries "Also called the terrace, the backyard or the porch" (shipped 2026-11-01), so the word was already measured and already vetted; it simply never reached the deck page, because `also_called_html()` (`ops/build_zone_pages.py`) only ever renders on room pages, and `zone_also_called_html()` only on zone pages. No deck-page generator, across all 20 rooms, has ever carried this heading. That is a structural gap in the deck-page template shape, not a missing synonym, and it is why seven other also-called entries in `ops/zone-also-called.json` already note a query routing through a `*-deck.html` page before a zone-page fix won the tie away from it.

Fixed narrowly, for this one room only (not a template-wide change, which is a larger workstream than this cycle's WIP allows): `ops/build_patio_or_deck_deck_page.py` now imports `also_called_html` from `build_zone_pages` and renders it under the "Six zones, real diagnosis, nothing invented" heading, in the page's light section rather than the dark hero (the function's own `color:var(--soft)` is illegible on the hero's dark background; checked `site/assets/css/site.css` directly before placing it).

Checked every remaining partial row against real content before concluding anything was unfixable: `shed` (routes to the Outdoor Storage Zone, whose own content is a deck box for cushions/games/covers, never a standalone shed, a different product); `cheap`/`diy`/`build`/`building` (the already-established generic-modifier/task-verb class); `gardening` (the Garden and Plant Care Zone's real content is pots and potting soil, never hand tools); `anking` (nonsense autocomplete, not a word); `plants` on "how to organize indoor plants" (wrong intent, this room is outdoor-only). One, "outdoor furniture seating storage" (unmatched `furniture`), was a genuine near-miss: the Outdoor Seating Zone already carries `furniture zone` as an also-called name (shipped 2026-11-05 PM check-in) and now ties the competing article page exactly (3 of 4 words each), losing the tie only on URL alphabetical order. Considered adding `storage` to the same zone's heading to break the tie, then rejected it: this room already has a real, distinct Outdoor Storage Zone, so calling the Outdoor Seating Zone "the storage zone" too would be a false, confusing name on a room that already has the honest word elsewhere, not a measured fix.

**Verified:** rebuilt `ops/build_patio_or_deck_deck_page.py`; grep-confirmed the heading renders on the real committed `site/patio-or-deck-deck.html`. `keyword_demand.py --rescore`: gap 112 to 109, partial 1256 to 1253, covered 1455 to 1461 (the 3 target queries plus 2 off-topic "back porch" rows that shared the same two-word content set, harmless). `keyword_demand_global.py --rescore`: gap 97 to 91, covered 1149 to 1155, partial unchanged. `room_guide_underperformance.py --room "Patio or Deck"`: 52% to 42% uncovered, 16 to 13 partial, 15 to 18 covered. `check_also_called_digital_collision_fast.py`, `test_room_also_called.py`, `test_gate_also_called_no_digital_collision.py` (5/5), `test_gate_zone_also_called_is_heading.py` (6/6) all clean; no new also-called name was added, so no new collision surface exists. `fix_dashes.py --check` (0/0), `check_urls.py` (235/235). `build_seo.py` rerun for the sitemap/content-hash refresh. `preflight.py --skip-tests` run fresh before and after: 0 FAIL both times.

## 115. Closed 2026-11-05, scheduled operator cycle: fixed the measurement-attribution bug behind most of entries 110-114's one-at-a-time room misattributions, instead of mining another single-word synonym

Before picking up the next room on `room_guide_underperformance.py`'s list, checked why "master bedroom storage" still scored to a Kids Bedroom zone despite `ops/room-also-called.json` recording "master bedroom" as a measured Primary Bedroom synonym since 2026-10-02 and `/rooms/primary-bedroom.html` already carrying the heading. Root cause in `ops/keyword_demand.py`'s `score_rows()`: `best_page`/`best_page_title` were always set from the title-surface winner, even on rows where `matched_on` already recorded the heading surface scored higher (or tied) and a different page earned the status. Measured the scope before fixing anything: 1402 of 2823 rows disagreed between the two surfaces, 778 of those in a different room entirely. `room_guide_candidates.py` and `room_guide_underperformance.py` both read `best_page` as "the page this query actually routes to", so each of those 778 was a live risk of a future cycle adding a dishonest also-called label to the wrong zone to "fix" a misattribution that was never really there.

Fixed at the root: `score_rows()` now decides one winning surface before using it anywhere downstream, and an exact tie between surfaces is now broken by the query's own room signal, the same rule `_tie_break()` already applies within one surface. `keyword_demand_global.py` reuses these functions directly, so one fix covers both corpora.

**Verified:** two new numbered cases in `ops/tests/test_keyword_demand.py` (case 20a/20b), fail-then-pass proved directly (`git stash` the fix, both new assertions failed with the exact wrong URLs the live bug produced; `git stash pop`, both passed). `keyword_demand.py --rescore` and `keyword_demand_global.py --rescore`: gap/partial/covered totals unchanged (112/1262/1449, 97/931/1142), as expected since the fix corrects attribution, not scores. Spot-checked the regenerated `ops/KEYWORD-DEMAND.md`: "back porch careers" now points at the Patio or Deck deck, not an unrelated article. `check_also_called_digital_collision_fast.py`, `test_gate_zone_also_called_is_heading.py` (6/6), `test_gate_also_called_no_digital_collision.py` (5/5), `test_room_also_called.py`, and the four `keyword-demand*` rescored/not-stale gate tests all still pass. `preflight.py --skip-tests` run fresh to completion: every gate passed, 39 warnings, all standing sandbox gaps, 0 FAIL.

No content, price or product page touched; this is a measurement-tool fix, not a site change, so `IndexNow` is not applicable.

**Next:** re-run `room_guide_underperformance.py` across all 20 rooms with corrected attribution before resuming the synonym-mining lane; some rooms' partial/gap lists will have shifted. Nothing from the 29 open issues is unblocked.

Pushed to main: `ops/keyword_demand.py`, `ops/tests/test_keyword_demand.py`, `ops/keyword-demand.json`, `ops/keyword-demand-global.json`, `ops/KEYWORD-DEMAND.md`, `ops/KEYWORD-DEMAND-GLOBAL.md`, `ops/NIGHTLY-LOG.md`, `STATUS.md`, this entry, dashboard files.

Pantry: the dry goods shelves' doorway sightline, the canned goods' riser depth, the snack zone's two-height split, the backstock zone's waist-height weight rule. **Went well:** treating a prior cycle's own "exhausted" conclusion as a claim to re-check rather than a fact, even though that cycle had explicitly audited the area already.

**Did not go well:** this is the third time this exact mistake shape has recurred (section 109 named it once, section 110 repeated it against a different tool, this entry found section 110 itself had repeated it a third time against a narrower scope than it claimed). The recurring root cause: "I checked X and found nothing more" gets written as "X is exhausted" without naming what X actually covered.

**Changing next cycle:** when closing a keyword-gap lane, name exactly what was checked (which tool, which rooms, which tiers) in the same sentence as the word "exhausted," so the next cycle can tell a narrow check from a complete one without re-deriving it.

**Next:** six rooms above, scoped and grounded, for the operator. 30 open `decision` GitHub issues, none from Phil, remain the standing queue otherwise.

## 104. Closed 2026-11-03, scheduled operator cycle: the "A37-shaped basement collision" sections 101-103 each named as the real next lever was checked directly, not re-cited, and does not hold up as described; a real but different gap found and fixed instead (Home Office "hard drive" digital noise)

**Verified the named claim before acting on it, per `CLAUDE.md` 5d.** Three consecutive entries (101, 102, 103) named "the 'outdoor storage' vs basement-article cross-page collision on Patio or Deck" as the real next lever, each time deferring it as bigger than that slot, without any of them calling the scorer directly against the real rows. Done this cycle: of the 9 "outdoor storage" queries in `ops/keyword-demand.json` plus 7 more in the global harvest, only ONE still has a genuine cross-page problem, "how to store clothes in outdoor storage" (both harvests), and it is a dead-even tie (0.75 heading-coverage each) between the real Patio or Deck Outdoor Storage Zone page and the basement article's unrelated FAQ ("What is the best way to store clothes in a basement?") plus its own "storage shelving" heading, not the one-sided misattribution the three prior entries described. Every other "outdoor storage" query in both harvests already resolves correctly to the Outdoor Storage Zone page (several fixed by sections 100-102's own "grilling zone"/cushions/snack-box work, which also reran the rescore this cycle's reading used).

**Checked whether the honest fix exists, and it does not.** The tie-break precedent (A31/A32/A40-A42) is to add the missing word only if the zone's own `content.json` purpose/done_looks_like genuinely supports it. Nowhere does it mention clothing, and the deck box's own described contents are patio accessories, not garments. Adding "clothes" to `zone-also-called.json` to win the tie would be the exact dishonest stretch `CLAUDE.md` section 6/8 forbids and A32's own "dinnerware" lesson warns against (a claim nobody searching for it actually makes about this page). **Correctly left unfixed, same precedent as Garage's car-organizer queries (section 102) and Workshop's paint queries**, not deferred as "bigger," because it the real size is one query this page cannot honestly answer.

"Hard drive" was simply never added to that list. Added it (`ops/room_guide_candidates.py`, shared by `room_guide_underperformance.py`), confirmed it does not false-match a genuine physical query ("how to organize a hardware closet" still excluded correctly, i.e. still matches, since "hardware" is a different word from "hard drive" and the regex only blocks the two-word phrase). This regex is explicitly documented as safe to widen (a false exclusion there "costs nothing," per its own comment), unlike `preflight.py`'s separate, narrower, hard-failing `_DIGITAL_CONTEXT_WORD`, which was deliberately left untouched.

**Turned into a test, per `CLAUDE.md` 10b.** Added a case to `ops/tests/test_room_guide_underperformance.py` (now 10/10) asserting a "hard drive" query scores 0 on-topic rows. ## 103. Closed 2026-11-03, scheduled operator cycle: a real CI FAIL from section 101's own fix, caught by `publish-image.yml` before it reached production, corrected rather than ignored

**The failure, confirmed before being treated as real, per `CLAUDE.md` 0.4.** Watched `checks.yml`/`publish-image.yml`/`deploy.yml` for section 101's push via a background monitor rather than assumed green. `publish-image.yml` run 41727990954 completed `failure` at its "Preflight, including generator ownership" step, the step that runs the full test suite this sandbox cannot (`gate_tests`, stalled here every cycle). Read the real log (`gh run view --job=... --log`), not a summary: `gate_also_called_no_digital_collision` FAILed, 2 gates failed total. `deploy.yml` never ran for this commit because the build never published; nothing section 101 shipped reached production in a broken state, confirmed directly rather than assumed, because the earlier bedroom-fix deploy (run 318) had already landed and this commit sat behind it, never promoted.

**The actual defect.** `gate_also_called_no_digital_collision` (built 2026-11-02, after an earlier cycle's own near-miss with "games") exists exactly because `zone_also_called_html()`'s heading is matched as a page-unscoped bag of words: a name added to fix one query can silently flip an unrelated query anywhere in the corpus if they share a word. Section 101's "grilling station" shared the word "station" with the unrelated digital-context query "desktop charging station organizer," a collision this sandbox's own local preflight runs never caught because they all used `--skip-tests` to route around the Chromium hang, and this specific check lives inside the full test suite, not the fast gate path. A real, if narrow, gap in this session's own verification discipline, not a false alarm.

**Fixed, not patched around.** Renamed the entry from "grilling station" to "grilling zone," the established `<word> zone` A-series naming precedent (A21/A23/A24/A32/A33/A34/A36) that exists for exactly this reason: it isolates the one missing content word without reusing a word already live in an unrelated digital query. Checked directly against the real gate function before shipping, not assumed: `check_also_called_no_digital_collision()` called live with the new name returns zero problems. `test_gate_also_called_no_digital_collision.py` (5/5) and `test_gate_zone_also_called_is_heading.py` (6/6) both pass against the real committed site.

**Verified.** `ops/keyword_demand.py --rescore`: "organize grilling station" still `covered` (`heading_coverage` 1.0, same page); the digital-context row "desktop charging station organizer" still routes to Family Room's charging zone, confirmed unaffected, not assumed. `fix_dashes.py --check` 0/0. `check_urls.py` 235/235. `audit_pages.py` 0 duplicates. `link_graph_report.py` 0 orphans. `affiliate.py --check` and `check_sellable.py` clean. `build_id.py --check` current.

**Went well:** watching the actual CI result for a push rather than treating "pushed" as "done" caught this before it reached production; the fix was already pointed at by the file's own precedent (A32's "dinnerware cabinet" lesson, the same shape) once the real cause was read.

**Did not go well, said plainly:** this session shipped a FAIL-worthy regression once this cycle, caught by CI rather than by this session's own local verification, because every local preflight run used `--skip-tests` to route around the documented Chromium hang, and `gate_also_called_no_digital_collision` lives inside that skipped suite. The fix shipped before production saw the bad version, which is the system working as designed, not luck, but the local verification gap is real.

**Changing next cycle:** when adding to `room-also-called.json` or `zone-also-called.json` specifically, run `check_also_called_no_digital_collision()` directly against the candidate name before shipping, the way this entry's fix did, rather than relying on `--skip-tests` preflight alone; that check is cheap (no Chromium, no network) and does not need the full suite to run standalone.

**Next:** unchanged from section 101: the A37-shaped basement collision on Patio or Deck's "outdoor storage" queries is the real next lever, then Garage/Hall Closet/Home Office/Kitchen by `room_guide_underperformance.py`'s ranking. 30 open `decision`/`blocked-on-art` GitHub issues, none from Phil since 2026-11-01/02; no push notification, nothing material changed on that front.

Pushed to main: `ops/zone-also-called.json`, `build_zone_pages.py`-regenerated files, `ops/keyword-demand.json`, `ops/KEYWORD-DEMAND.md`, this entry, dashboard files. No price or product touched; no new page, so `IndexNow` does not apply.

## 100. Closed 2026-11-03, scheduled operator cycle: built the next-lever tool the 20:4x PM check-in named, now that the room-guide gap-filling lane is closed, and used it to close one real coverage gap

**Checked before building anything, per `CLAUDE.md` 5c/5d.** Unshallowed genuinely this time (watched the process reach real completion, `is-shallow-repository` false, not assumed from elapsed time), attached cleanly, rebased one trivial checkpoint commit onto a concurrent cycle's push rather than force anything. Independently re-verified the exhaustion claim rather than trusting the fifth confirmation: `room_guide_candidates.py` printed nothing (0 guide-less rooms), `cold_read_ledger.py --next` read 306 of 306 ledgered with 0 un-ledgered candidates, `ops/state.json` showed a fresh (not carried-forward) Stripe read still at 1 sale/$19 all time and $0 this month, 30 GitHub issues re-pulled sorted by `updated_at` descending showed nothing newer than 2026-11-01, and `inbox_agent.py --apply` again found no mail credential in this sandbox. All of it held.

**The named next lever, built rather than deferred a sixth time.** The 20:4x PM check-in's own handoff named a real, different question this repository had not yet answered with one method: `room_guide_candidates.py` only ever asked "does this room have a guide," and that is now true for all 20, so it goes silent. It never asked "how well does an already-shipped guide, plus its zone and deck pages, actually answer the real search demand that is on-topic for that room." Built `ops/room_guide_underperformance.py`: same matching rules as its sibling (`best_page` routing over the sparse `room` field, the on-topic/digital-context filters, the US-plus-global dedup guard that prevents a shared query counting twice), extended with one more route a room can answer through that the old tool never needed, its own guide article. Ranks every room by the share of its on-topic, routed demand that is still `gap` or `partial` rather than `covered`.

**Tested before trusting it.** `ops/tests/test_room_guide_underperformance.py`, 9 checks: the room list is derived live from `content.json`, not hand-copied; every guided room resolves a `guide_path` and an unguided one does not; the new guide-article route actually matches, the old deck/zone routes still do too, the dedup guard holds, off-topic and digital-context rows are still excluded, and the shortfall ratio is 0 when nothing is left uncovered. Fail-then-pass proved directly: reverting the guide-article match in `room_rows()` turned 3 of the 9 red, confirmed, then restored.

**Ran it, read the output rather than trusted the ranking, and verified the one candidate that held up.** Workshop ranked worst (61% uncovered) but its own top rows turned out to be a "paint" word-overlap collision, not real workshop demand: `/zones/workshop-the-finishing-and-chemical-zone.html`'s own `<title>` says "workshop paint and chemical storage," which pulls in craft-paint queries ("acrylic paint storage organizer") that have nothing to do with wood finish or house paint, the same false-attribution shape several A-series fixes already corrected for other words; left alone rather than built around, since fixing it honestly would need deciding whether this site serves craft-paint storage at all, not adding a synonym. Checked the actual top "gap" rows (true 0.0-coverage misses, a stronger signal than a partial match) across every room instead: most were autocomplete noise or stray verb-sense collisions ("how to paint a metal storage container" is about painting an object, not about paint storage), but Primary Bedroom's "organizing ideas for bedrooms" (US tier, rank 11, `gap`, `unmatched_words: ["bedrooms"]`) was a clean, verifiable miss, read directly against the raw JSON before acting on it: the room's guide article title says "bedroom" (singular), and the scorer matches exact words, not stems, the identical shape A34 (nappy/nappies) and A36 (craft/crafts) already fixed.

**The smallest honest fix, grounded in the page's own already-stated content.** `site/articles/how-to-declutter-a-bedroom.html` is hand-authored (no `ops/build_*.py` writes its body, confirmed before editing it). ## 90. Closed 2026-11-03, scheduled operator cycle: verified and closed the one real candidate the 03:4x PM check-in named, the global tier's last on-topic gap row

**Checked before acting, per CLAUDE.md 5c/5d.** The prior check-in named "how to organise belongings" (rank 11, global gap, 0.0 coverage) as the one genuine on-topic miss left in 98 mostly-noise gap rows, but had not verified it against real content. Ran it live: `content_words()` already folds `organise` to `organize` (the 2026-10-23 fix) and deliberately excludes it as a task-verb `FORMAT_WORD`, so the only real content word is `belongings`, and it appeared on no page's title or heading at all. Confirmed the row was not stale scoring: live `best_page()`/`best_by()` against the real site both returned 0.0 for `/about.html`, matching the stored row exactly.

**The real gap, and the smallest honest fix.** "Belongings" is a whole-house synonym for the thing this entire product organizes, not a room- or zone-level term, so the right target is `where-to-start-decluttering.html`, the one article already about organizing a whole house rather than one room, hand-authored (confirmed no `ops/build_*.py` generator writes its body before editing it, only `build_articles.py`'s/`build_zone_pages.py`'s own "Keep reading" lists reference it). Added one genuine FAQ question in its existing voice, "How do I organize all my belongings, not just declutter them?", answered from the page's own already-stated method (a zone is not finished until everything staying in it has an assigned spot), in both the visible `<h3>`/`<p>` and the matching `FAQPage` JSON-LD entry. No invented claim, no new statistic, no price or product touched.

## 89. Closed 2026-11-03, scheduled operator cycle: a real blind spot in `room_guide_candidates.py` found while verifying a handoff, before shipping a guide the evidence did not actually support

**The handoff, checked before acting on it, not after.** The 05:4x PM check-in named Entryway as the next unblocked content item, citing "23 unmet rows... ties Mudroom... larger total demand (50 rows vs 43)". That count used the sparse `room` field on `ops/keyword-demand.json` rows, the exact method `room_guide_candidates.py`'s own docstring already names as deprecated (881 of 2808 rows carry it, the rest do not, and it was the source of three earlier wrong gap counts in sections 82/83/84). Ran `room_guide_candidates.py` itself first, per `CLAUDE.md` 5c/5d: it said Entryway has 0 on-topic gap and only 3 partial, a much weaker case than the PM's claim.

**Neither number was trustworthy as stated, so both were checked against the real file.** Reading the `room`-field rows directly: 23 of 50 are `gap`/`partial`, and nearly all are genuinely on-topic ("entryway closet ideas", "how to declutter entryway", "narrow entryway organization ideas"), not noise. But `room_guide_candidates.py`'s own `room_rows()` only matches `best_page == "/entryway-deck.html"` or a `/zones/entryway-*` prefix, by design (its docstring explains why it prefers `best_page` over the sparse field). Checking what those 23 rows actually route to: 53 of them land on `/deck/entryway-print-and-play.html`, a second, differently shaped deck URL this one room ships (a print-and-play page under `/deck/`, not `{room}-deck.html`) that `room_rows()` was never taught to recognise. So the tool was silently blind to 80 real Entryway rows, 30 of them on-topic, **0 of them `gap`**, because that page already answers most of them (many `covered`, the rest `partial`).

**Fixed the tool, not the symptom.** `room_rows()` now also matches `/deck/{slug}-print-and-play.html`. Fail-then-pass proved: reverted only the source change (kept the new test), confirmed `test_room_guide_candidates.py` FAILs on the missing match, reapplied, confirmed 7/7 pass. Re-ran `room_guide_candidates.py` on the real corpus after fixing: Entryway now scores 0 on-topic gap, 15 partial (up from 3), 38 on-topic of 121 routed rows (up from 8 of 41). Entryway has no second shipped room with the same URL shape today, so this was not special-cased to one slug; the pattern is matched generally.

**The decision, now on real evidence rather than either prior count.** Entryway does not get a guide this cycle: 0 gap either way, and the corrected reading shows its existing print-and-play deck page already covers most of the organizing queries that exist for it (several `covered` at full score), the opposite of a real gap. Declining for the same reason section 84's Family Room decision did: the method this repository now trusts says no, and the PM's naive count that said otherwise was itself the thing worth checking, not the thing to act on. Guest Bedroom (11 partial, still the highest of the genuinely guide-less rooms, confirmed unaffected by this fix since it ships no print-and-play variant) remains the next weakest-case candidate if that lane is picked up again; no room currently clears the Workshop-class bar (31+34) that justified a guide outright.

**Verified.** `python ops/tests/test_room_guide_candidates.py` 7/7. `python ops/fix_dashes.py --check` on both changed files, 0/0. Python syntax parsed directly on both files before relying on them. No content, price or product touched; no new page shipped, so `IndexNow` does not apply. `ops/preflight.py` run fresh this cycle reached `gate_tests` clean (0 FAIL on everything before it, `cycle_signal.py --check` confirms SAME against this snapshot) before hitting the same documented sandbox Chromium hang; killed cleanly, no orphaned process left behind.
